### 2026-11-01: detailed Part I spine and appendix design
Every change below is forced by the concept DAG (ledgers/CONCEPTS.md) or by the section-size rule, unless stated otherwise.
1. **Chapter order 4 ↔ 6.** Linear response moves from Chap. 6 to Chap. 4; lattice dynamics becomes Chap. 5 and electron–phonon coupling
   Chap. 6. *Why:* the old order used linear-response concepts before defining them: DFPT (old 4.3, 5.3) is static linear response; ε_∞
   (old 4.4) needs the microscopic dielectric function; the screened electron–phonon vertex (old 5.2) needs screening; the phonon
   self-energy (old 5.4) needs Green's functions; phonon-assisted absorption (old 5.5) needs ε₂. Linear response needs only Chaps. 2–3, so it
   can come first. The new order also mirrors Part II (electronic, lattice, mixed).
2. **Born–Oppenheimer split.** Old 5.1 is split: the approximation, the energy surface and κ_BO open lattice dynamics as new 5.1; the
   non-adiabatic couplings and Migdal's theorem become new 6.1. *Why:* force constants (5.2) are derivatives of the Born–Oppenheimer
   surface. T4 still opens in 5.1. Chap. 5 now has 7 sections.
3. **Merge old 3.5 and 3.7** into 3.5 (plane waves, pseudopotentials, core states, relativistic corrections for cores); valence spin–orbit
   splitting moves to 3.3. *Why:* old 3.7 alone was below the 3,000-word floor, and core relativistic effects concern the same core region as
   all-electron reconstruction. Chap. 3 has 6 sections.
4. **η(v_min) moves from 1.2 to 1.3.** *Why:* it needs v_min, derived in 1.3.
5. **"Brillouin-zone scale" in 1.3** is replaced by the interatomic scale 2π/a; the comparison with the zone is made in 3.1, which now
   advances T1.
6. **Two-stage definitions.** σ̄_e and F_DM are defined in 1.4 (with the undergraduate Born approximation the reader is assumed to know)
   and derived in 2.2; the band gap is qualitative in 1.5 and precise in 3.3; the static lattice is a stated working assumption in 3.1,
   derived as the clamped-nuclei limit in 5.1. Each pair is two rows in CONCEPTS.
7. **New content within established physics.** 3.2 introduces the density vertex ρ_mn(k, q) (every electronic rate needs it); 4.2 derives
   the Born rate off any many-body target (Van Hove), which Chap. 8 later generalizes; 4.4 defines ε_∞ microscopically for 5.5; 4.5 adds the
   Wannier–Mott exciton; 6.1 adds the kicked-atom survival probability as the bridge to Chap. 12; 7.2 adds the conserving response check.
8. **Titles** are cut to short noun phrases (e.g. "Born approximation and form factors", "Silicon, germanium and gallium arsenide").
9. **Tags.** Chap. 8 and Chaps. 9–22 read "BLOCKED (pending final merge)"; the editor unblocks them. Their content is unchanged.
10. **Appendices.** A–D designed at section level; D lists the 19 Part I derivations (D.1–D.19), each with a two-way pointer to its
    section (checked by script); E reorganised into E.1–E.12 with the order in which Part I needs them; **E.9** (magnetic and
    superconducting parameters) and **E.10** (defects) are new, because 7.1, 7.2 and 7.4 introduce computed quantities with no E section
    to point to. E′ and F are unchanged.
11. **Threads.** T2 opens in 4.4 (was 6.4), T3 in 4.3 (was 6.3); T5 advances in 6.5 (was 5.5); Part I advances added (THREADS.md).
12. **Budget.** Part I: 39 sections, 184,000 words plus 6,300 for chapter outlines and summaries. Book total revised to about 671,000
    words (see Budget), because the earlier figure did not budget appendices or summaries.
13. **Checks run.** (a) Every SPINE prerequisite of a Part I entry points to an earlier section or to READER.md: 0 violations. (b) Every
    CONCEPTS row is used only after its defining section: 134 rows, 0 violations. (e) Every A.n and C.n
    "Used by" list matches the pointers in the entries: 0 mismatches. (c) Every D.n and E.n named in a Part I entry appears in
    the appendix list with that section, and vice versa: 0 mismatches. (d) A grep over Chaps. 8–22 finds no Part I section number, so the
    renumbering breaks no reference.
14. **For the editor (outside this pass's edit scope):** the SPINE status line at the top and the E′ description contain process wording
    ("hostile review", "the campaign's specifications"); they are internal files, but the E′ text should be rewritten before E′ is briefed.
    The NOTATION clash list (26 items) needs the editor's confirmation before the first writer brief. Equation numbers marked "eq. to fix"
    in entries and in CLAIMS must be read from full text by writers. Every arXiv identifier in Part I was checked against the arXiv API
    (2026-11-01); journal volume/page data of classic papers and textbook chapter/section numbers were entered from standard knowledge
    and must be confirmed at briefing. The only equation numbers given are those already quoted from full text in the planning material.
- Chap. 16 retitled "Novel targets" (user asked whether "Exotic targets" is more colloquial; "novel targets/materials" is the standard field phrase, and "exotic" usually labels dark-matter models, not detectors).
